  `offsets.retention.minutes` expires them, is still listed by ListGroups, is described as `Empty` with its
  protocol type and no members, and answers JoinGroup normally so that a new member can take it over. A 0.9.0.1
  coordinator dropped such a group at once and answered `Dead` for it, which made "everybody left" and "never
  existed" indistinguishable. DescribeGroups still answers `Dead` with the error code 0 for a group that really
  does not exist, and `ClientHost` still arrives in Java's `InetAddress` notation, with a leading slash
  (`/172.18.0.1`). → "DescribeGroups API (key 15, v0 and v1)".

* **(0.10) The coordinator waits the `RebalanceTimeout` of JoinGroup v1, not the session timeout.** A member that
  joined with `session = 30000, rebalance = 4000` and then went silent delayed the join of the next member by
  exactly 4.00 s; the same experiment with **v0** frames and `session = 10000` took 10.00 s, because
  `JoinGroupRequest.rebalanceTimeout` falls back to the session timeout for a v0 frame. The rebalance timeout is
  bounded by nothing - `group.max.session.timeout.ms` of the broker applies to the *session* timeout alone, so
  `rebalance = 300000` is accepted on a broker whose maximum is 60000, and `rebalance = 0` is accepted too.
  → "`RebalanceTimeout`, the wire half of `max.poll.interval.ms` (v1, Kafka 0.10.1, KIP-62)".

**Topic administration (the controller apis)**

* **Only the controller can create or delete a topic**; every other broker answers 41 (`NotController`) for a topic
  it is asked about. The controller is the `ControllerId` of a Metadata v1/v2 answer, and a 41 means that id is
  stale. → "CreateTopics API (key 19, v0, v1 and v2)".
* **An unparsable option *value* is answered -1, an unknown option *name* 40.** `LogConfig.validate` throws Kafka's
  own `InvalidConfigurationException` (an `ApiException`, code 40) for a name it does not know but the plain
  `ConfigException` for a value it cannot parse, and `Errors.forException` has no code for that one.
  **(0.11) The message text of the first case changed**: `LogConfig.validateNames()` @ 0.11.0.3 throws
  `Unknown topic config name: no.such.option`, where 0.10.2.2 let the generic `AbstractConfig` throw
  `Unknown Log configuration no.such.option.` — same code, same shape of answer, different `ErrorMessage`. A client
  that matched on the text broke; matching on the code did not.
  → "CreateTopics API (key 19, v0, v1 and v2)".
* **`timeout = 0` answers 7 (`RequestTimedOut`) and does the work anyway**: the topic is in the metadata about 50 ms
  later, and a deletion finishes just as well. An assignment that names a broker id the cluster does not have is
  accepted and answered 7 as well - the topic is written to ZooKeeper and never gets a leader.
  → "CreateTopics API (key 19, v0, v1 and v2)".
* **A DeleteTopics request with an empty topic array is answered with an empty array**, by the controller and by
  every follower alike: `KafkaApis.handleDeleteTopicsRequest` maps over the topics of the *request*. That is why the
  controller probe of `AdminClient::findController()` names one topic whose name can never be legal instead.
  Deleting a topic that does not exist answers 3 and creates nothing. → "DeleteTopics API (key 20, v0 and v1)".

**(0.11) Records and configuration (the admin apis Kafka 0.11 added)**

* **(0.11) DeleteRecords goes to the LEADER of a partition, not to the controller.** It is the only one of the five
  admin apis that is split per partition leader, like Produce and Fetch, and it reports the new low watermark of
  every partition - the same number that `Offsets(EARLIEST)` and the `log_start_offset` of a Fetch v5 answer carry.
  A consumer that was reading below the new watermark gets 1 (OffsetOutOfRange) on its next fetch.
  → "DeleteRecords API (key 21, v0)".
* **(0.11) An offset above the high watermark is 1, and so is every negative offset except -1.**
  `ReplicaManager.deleteRecordsOnLocalLog` resolves -1 to the high watermark and then rejects anything below zero
  with an `OffsetOutOfRangeException`, so `-2` - "earliest" in the Offsets api - is answered **1** and not 42.
  Deleting at or below the watermark the partition already has is **not** an error: it answers 0 and the current
  watermark, because `Log.maybeIncrementLogStartOffset` only moves it forward. → "DeleteRecords API (key 21, v0)".
* **(0.11) DeleteRecords does not create a topic that does not exist**, although the broker runs with
  `auto.create.topics.enable=true`: `KafkaApis` only asks `metadataCache.contains(topic)` and answers 3 for every
  requested partition of it. → "DeleteRecords API (key 21, v0)".
* **(0.11) DescribeConfigs answers a topic that does not exist with the DEFAULTS and the error code 0.**
  `AdminUtils.fetchEntityConfig` returns empty properties for a missing ZooKeeper node and the broker merges them
  with its own log defaults, so the api says nothing about whether a topic exists. An option name the broker does
  not know is dropped from the answer just as silently - the entry list simply comes back shorter, with the error
  code 0. → "DescribeConfigs API (key 32, v0)".
* **(0.11) A broker resource is answered by that broker alone**, with 42 and `Unexpected broker id, expected 0, but
  received 7` everywhere else, and a broker id that is not a number with 42 and `Broker id must be an integer, but
  it is: nope`. Every entry of a broker resource is flagged `ReadOnly`, and the value of a `PASSWORD` option is
  always `null` on the wire. → "DescribeConfigs API (key 32, v0)".
* **(0.11) AlterConfigs REPLACES the configuration of a topic.** An option that was set and is not in the request is
  reset to its default, and an empty entry array resets all of them - the broker builds a fresh `Properties` and
  overwrites the ZooKeeper node. Any broker serves the request; the controller is not involved.
  → "AlterConfigs API (key 33, v0)".
* **(0.11) AlterConfigs refuses a broker resource** with 42 and `AlterConfigs is only supported for topics, but
  resource type is BROKER`, whether `ValidateOnly` is set or not - dynamic broker configuration is Kafka 1.1
  (KIP-226) - and it answers a topic that does not exist and a `null` config value with **-1**, because
  `AdminOperationException` and the `NullPointerException` of `Properties.setProperty` are not `ApiException`s. An
  unparsable option **value** is 42 here and -1 through CreateTopics, an unknown option **name** is 40 in both.
  → "AlterConfigs API (key 33, v0)".

**Quotas, transport and the controller**

* **A quota never rejects anything**: the broker appends or reads and delays the *answer*, and reports the delay in
  the `ThrottleTime` of Produce v1 / Fetch v1. A throttled Fetch answer therefore arrives late, which a consumer
  with a short `fetch.max.wait.ms` can mistake for a timeout of its own — `Client::dispatch()` waits at most twice
  the `fetch.max.wait.ms` it sent. A fire-and-forget batch (`RequiredAcks = 0`) is throttled as well and never
  learns about it. → "Quotas and throttle time".
* **ControlledShutdown for a broker id the controller does not know answers 8** (`BrokerNotAvailable`), where
  0.8.2.2 leaked -1: `handleError()` maps `e.getClass` instead of `e.getCause`. It answers that for every version
  it is sent with. → "ControlledShutdown API (key 7, v0 and v1)".
* **(0.10) Wrong SASL credentials have no error code**: the broker closes the connection during the token exchange,
  without an answer - `SaslAuthenticate` (key 36), which would carry one, is Kafka 1.0. A mechanism the broker has
  not enabled is answered 33 with the list of the enabled ones, and *then* the socket is closed; an ordinary request
  as the first frame of a SASL listener, and a second handshake on an authenticated connection, are closed without
  an answer. **ApiVersions is answered before the handshake** (KIP-35), which is what lets a client ask what the
  broker speaks before it can authenticate. → "SASL/PLAIN".
* **The TLS handshake is bounded by the connection timeout of the stream**, not by `request.timeout.ms`, and
  OpenSSL 3 refuses `ssl.protocol = TLSv1_1` alone (`no protocols available`) even though the broker offers it,
  because its default security level rejects TLS below 1.2. → "Transport security (SSL)".

**The container itself**

* **(0.11) The `server.properties` shipped with Kafka 0.11 ends without a trailing newline.** `start.sh` of
  `docker/kafka-0.11.0.3/` appends one before it writes the settings of this repository into the file; without it
  the first appended line is glued to the last shipped one and the broker starts with a property it never asked for.
* **(0.11) The transaction state log needs `transaction.state.log.replication.factor=1` and
  `transaction.state.log.min.isr=1`** on a one-broker cluster. Without them `__transaction_state` cannot be created
  and every transactional request ends in 15 (`GroupCoordinatorNotAvailable`), long after the api itself parsed
  fine. The container of this line sets both.
* **A 0.11.0.3 broker keeps reading and writing the older message formats.** A topic created with
  `--config message.format.version=0.10.0` or `0.9.0` stores the format that version implies, and a Fetch below the
  version that understands the newer format is answered **down-converted**. That is what keeps the wire vectors of
  the three lines below replayable against this broker, and it costs the broker a copy of the log region per
  answer. → "What the broker converts, and when".
* **(0.11) A down-converted `LogAppendTime` batch stamps the timestamp type on every inner message.** A topic with
  `message.timestamp.type=LogAppendTime` and the default `message.format.version=0.11.0` stores a **record batch
  v2**, where the timestamp type is an attribute of the batch; converting it down to format v1 for a Fetch v2/v3
  rebuilds the set with `AbstractRecords.convertRecordBatch()`, and a magic 1 builder writes that timestamp type -
  and the append time - into **every** message it appends. The inner messages of the answer therefore carry the
  LogAppendTime bit and the append time, where a 0.10.2.2 broker, whose log held the client's own v1 set, left the
  inner attributes and `CreateTime` values untouched and set the bit on the wrapper alone. Either way a reader has
  to take the timestamp of the wrapper for the whole batch, which is what this client does.
  (`MessageFormatV1Test::testALogAppendTimeWrapperCarriesTheTimestampOfTheWholeCompressedBatch`.)

**The versions that KIP-124 raised (0.11)**

* **(0.11) A 0.11.0.3 broker never fills the `error_message` of GroupCoordinator v1.** The field is a
  `NULLABLE_STRING` that version 1 added right behind the error code, and every constructor of
  `FindCoordinatorResponse` that the broker uses sets it to `null` — `this.errorMessage = null` in both public
  constructors, and `toStruct()` writes whatever that field holds. Verified on the container for a successful
  lookup, for a lookup answered 15 while `__consumer_offsets` was being created, and for a transactional id: `ff ff`
  in every case. A client must therefore act on `error_code` alone and treat the message as a hint that a later
  release may fill in. (`ThrottleTimeApiTest::testTheErrorMessageOfACoordinatorAnswerIsAlwaysNull`.)
* **(0.11) An unknown `coordinator_type` costs the connection instead of producing an error code.** The type is
  parsed into an enum before the handler ever runs, and `FindCoordinatorRequest$CoordinatorType.forId` throws for
  anything but 0 and 1; `RequestChannel.Request` wraps that in an `InvalidRequestException` and `SocketServer`
  closes the channel, exactly as for an unknown api key. The broker log says

  ```
  ERROR Closing socket for 172.18.0.2:9092-172.18.0.1:42964 because of error (kafka.network.Processor)
  org.apache.kafka.common.errors.InvalidRequestException: Error getting request for apiKey: 10 and apiVersion: 1
  Caused by: java.lang.IllegalArgumentException: Unknown coordinator type received: 7
  ```

  so the error code 42 (`InvalidRequest`) with an `error_message` — which is what the field looks made for — is
  never what a client gets for it. `GroupCoordinatorRequest::COORDINATOR_TYPE_GROUP` and `…_TRANSACTION` are the
  only two values a client may send. (`ThrottleTimeApiTest::testAnUnknownCoordinatorTypeClosesTheConnection`.)
* **(0.11) The first lookup of a transactional id creates `__transaction_state`**, exactly as the first group
  lookup creates `__consumer_offsets`, and is answered with 15 (`GroupCoordinatorNotAvailable`) while that happens.
  On the container the topic appears with the 50 partitions of `transaction.state.log.num.partitions` and the
  replication factor 1 the image configures, and the next lookup answers 0 with broker 0. The id itself is neither
  created nor registered by the lookup — the broker only hashes the key onto a partition of that topic — so a
  lookup of an id that was never used is a normal question with a normal answer.
* **(0.11) Metadata v4 with `allow_auto_topic_creation = false` really creates nothing.** A request naming a topic
  the cluster does not have is answered with the topic error code **3** (`UnknownTopicOrPartition`) and an empty
  partition list, and `kafka-topics.sh --list` afterwards does not show the topic — where the same request with the
  flag `true`, or any version below 4, answers the code 5 (`LeaderNotAvailable`) and leaves a fresh topic behind.
  This is what lets `AdminClient::describeTopics()` be a question rather than a side effect.
  (`MetadataApiTest::testAVersionFourRequestWithoutAutoCreationDoesNotCreateTheTopic`.)
* **(0.11) Offsets v2 with `read_committed` answers the last stable offset, not the log end offset.** Without an
  open transaction the two are equal, which is why the vectors `offsets.response.v2.latest` and
  `offsets.response.v2.read-committed` carry the same offset; the difference only shows on a partition with an
  in-flight transaction, where the LSO stops at the first record of it. A consumer that fetches with
  `read_committed` has to list its end offsets with the same level, or it waits for records it will never be shown.
* **(0.11) The throttle time of every one of the fourteen apis is 0 on this container**, because it configures no
  quota at all. A non-zero one is only produced deliberately, by `QuotaThrottleTest`, which sets a
  `producer_byte_rate` for a client id of its own.
**A fetch is answered in whole batches, so it can carry records below the offset it asked for.** That was already
true of a *compressed* message set, and the message format v2 makes it the normal case: every produce call writes
one batch per topic-partition, and a fetch that starts inside one is answered with the whole batch. A partition
whose records were deleted up to the offset 2 of a five-record batch therefore answers a fetch from the offset 2
with all five records; dropping the ones below the position is the job of the consumer
(`KafkaConsumer::collectRecords()` does it, `Client::fetch()` reports what the broker sent).

**A Produce v3 request with a message set closes the connection.** `ProduceRequest.parse` @ 0.11.0.3 refuses a
record set whose magic is below 2 for a version 3 request, and the broker answers by dropping the socket, not with
an error code; the versions 0 to 2 accept every magic, the message format v2 included. See the table in the Produce
section above.

**A record with headers can not be down-converted.** A Fetch below version 4 of a partition whose records carry
headers answers that partition with the error code -1, `UnknownServerError`, and an empty record set: the magic 1
builder of the broker refuses the headers. See the Fetch section above.

**A Produce v3 answer is a Produce v2 answer.** `PRODUCE_RESPONSE_V3` **is** `PRODUCE_RESPONSE_V2` in
`Protocol.java` @ 0.11.0.3: version 3 of the api changed the *request* only. The `log_start_offset` that a Produce
answer eventually got is Kafka 1.0 (Produce v5), so a client that reads a v3 answer with a partition entry of five
fields runs past the end of the frame. Verified: the answer of `produce.request.v3` is 50 bytes and ends with its
`ThrottleTime` right behind the `LogAppendTime`.

**`last_stable_offset` and `aborted_transactions` are the "not asked" answer of a `read_uncommitted` fetch, not a
state of the partition.** A Fetch v4/v5 with `isolation_level = 0` is answered with `LastStableOffset = -1` and the
element count **-1** of a null `AbortedTransactions` array, whatever the partition holds; with `isolation_level = 1`
the same partition answers the real LSO - the high water mark when no transaction ever touched it - and an array
that is present and *empty*. `log_start_offset` is reported for both levels, because it is a property of the log.

**A `read_committed` fetch is not filtered by the broker.** The answer carries the records of an aborted
transaction and the control batch with its `ABORT` marker; `aborted_transactions` only says which producer to drop
the records of. `fetch.response.v5.aborted` holds both batches.

**A fetch never crosses a segment boundary.** Observed while capturing the aborted transaction: when the abort
marker of the coordinator landed in a segment of its own, a fetch from the offset 0 answered with the data batch
alone although the last stable offset was two offsets further on, and the marker needed a second fetch. `Log.read`
reads from a single segment, so an answer that stops below the high water mark says nothing about the log.

**Time-based retention deletes a segment by the largest timestamp it holds, not by the time it was written.** A
batch produced with a `CreateTime` of 1600000000000 (2020-09-13) into a container whose clock says 2026 is deleted
by the next retention check, `log_start_offset` moves and the records are gone - which is why every capture and
every integration test of this line stamps its records with the current clock.

* **`EndTxn` is idempotent for a commit but not for an abort.** A transactional id in `CompleteCommit`
  that is asked to commit again is answered **0**; the same id asked to *abort* is answered **48**
  (`InvalidTxnState`). The state table of `TransactionCoordinator.handleEndTransaction` @ 0.11.0.3 accepts the
  result a completed transaction already has and refuses the other one, so a producer that lost the answer of its
  `EndTxn` may repeat it, and only that.
* **A `read_committed` fetch is not filtered by the broker.** It is bounded by the last stable offset and it
  carries an `aborted_transactions` array, but the aborted records and the ABORT control batch are in the answer:
  a partition with one aborted transaction of two records answers `hw = 3`, `lso = 3`, one aborted transaction,
  two batches and two records. The client drops them (see "Transactions"). A `read_uncommitted` fetch of the same
  partition answers `lso = -1` and `aborted_transactions = null`, and carries exactly the same bytes.
* **While a transaction is open, a `read_committed` fetch of its partition answers nothing at all.** The high
  watermark has moved past the records - a `read_uncommitted` reader sees them - but the last stable offset is
  still the first offset of the transaction, and the broker cuts the answer there: measured `hw = 2`, `lso = 0`,
  zero bytes of records.
* **The errors of AddPartitionsToTxn and TxnOffsetCommit are per partition, including the ones that are not about
  a partition.** Neither api has a top-level error code, so 47, 48, 49 and 51 - all statements about the
  transactional id - arrive repeated on every partition of the answer.
* **WriteTxnMarkers is the only answer of 0.11 without a `throttle_time_ms`**, and the unsecured container serves
  it to an ordinary client, which really appends the control batch it asks for.

## Error codes

The authority is `clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/protocol/Errors.java` @ 0.11.0.3, which ends at
`55`. The 0.10 line added the codes in three steps (**32-35** with 0.10.0, **36-42** with 0.10.1, **43-44** with
0.10.2) and Kafka 0.11 added **45-55** in one: 45-47 for the idempotent producer, 48-53 for the transactions and
54-55 for the ACL apis. Nothing above `55` exists on this branch — `KAFKA_STORAGE_ERROR` (56) is Kafka 1.0 — and
`KafkaException::fromCode()` answers an unknown code with `UnknownErrorException`, which is what a client should do
with a code it has never heard of.

`R` marks the codes **this client** treats as retriable (`Common\Errors\RetriableException`), which is the set the
Java client marks with `RetriableException` and its subclass `InvalidMetadataException`, with the one documented
exception of the code 46 in the notes below. `Network\RetryPolicy` refreshes the metadata and sends the request
again for the codes 3, 5, 6 and 13.

| Code | Name in `Errors.java`        | Exception class (as on `main`)          | R | Meaning                                                    |
|------|------------------------------|-----------------------------------------|---|------------------------------------------------------------|
| -1   | UNKNOWN                      | `UnknownErrorException`                 |   | Unexpected server error                                    |
| 0    | NONE                         | -                                       |   | Success                                                    |
| 1    | OFFSET_OUT_OF_RANGE          | `OffsetOutOfRangeException`             |   | The requested offset is outside the range kept by the server |
| 2    | CORRUPT_MESSAGE              | `CorruptMessageException`               | R | Message CRC does not match, the message is corrupt         |
| 3    | UNKNOWN_TOPIC_OR_PARTITION   | `UnknownTopicOrPartitionException`      | R | This server does not host this topic-partition             |
| 4    | (InvalidFetchSize)           | `InvalidFetchSizeException`             |   | Only in `ErrorMapping.scala`; `Errors.java` leaves 4 free  |
| 5    | LEADER_NOT_AVAILABLE         | `LeaderNotAvailableException`           | R | No leader for the partition, an election is in progress    |
| 6    | NOT_LEADER_FOR_PARTITION     | `NotLeaderForPartitionException`        | R | This broker is not the leader for that partition           |
| 7    | REQUEST_TIMED_OUT            | `RequestTimedOutException`              | R | The request exceeded its timeout on the broker             |
| 8    | BROKER_NOT_AVAILABLE         | `BrokerNotAvailableException`           |   | Broker-internal error code, should not reach a client      |
| 9    | REPLICA_NOT_AVAILABLE        | `ReplicaNotAvailableException`          |   | A requested replica is not part of the assignment any more |
| 10   | MESSAGE_TOO_LARGE            | `MessageTooLargeException`              |   | The message is larger than `message.max.bytes`             |
| 11   | STALE_CONTROLLER_EPOCH       | `StaleControllerEpochException`         |   | Controller moved to another broker                         |
| 12   | OFFSET_METADATA_TOO_LARGE    | `OffsetMetadataTooLargeException`       |   | The committed offset metadata exceeds the configured maximum |
| 13   | NETWORK_EXCEPTION            | `NetworkException`                      | R | The server disconnected before a response was received     |
| 14   | GROUP_LOAD_IN_PROGRESS       | `GroupLoadInProgressException`          | R | The coordinator is still loading the state of the group    |
| 15   | GROUP_COORDINATOR_NOT_AVAILABLE | `GroupCoordinatorNotAvailableException` | R | The `__consumer_offsets` topic is not available yet     |
| 16   | NOT_COORDINATOR_FOR_GROUP    | `NotCoordinatorForGroupException`       | R | This broker is not the coordinator of that group           |
| 17   | INVALID_TOPIC_EXCEPTION      | `InvalidTopicException`                 |   | The topic name is invalid, or is an internal topic         |
| 18   | RECORD_LIST_TOO_LARGE        | `RecordListTooLargeException`           |   | The message set exceeds the maximum accepted size          |
| 19   | NOT_ENOUGH_REPLICAS          | `NotEnoughReplicasException`            | R | Fewer in-sync replicas than `min.insync.replicas`          |
| 20   | NOT_ENOUGH_REPLICAS_AFTER_APPEND | `NotEnoughReplicasAfterAppendException` | R | Appended, but not replicated to enough replicas        |
| 21   | INVALID_REQUIRED_ACKS        | `InvalidRequiredAcksException`          |   | The Produce request asked for an impossible `RequiredAcks` |
| 22   | ILLEGAL_GENERATION           | `IllegalGenerationException`            |   | The generation id of the group is not the current one      |
| 23   | INCONSISTENT_GROUP_PROTOCOL  | `InconsistentGroupProtocolException`    |   | The member offers no protocol the other members support    |
| 24   | INVALID_GROUP_ID             | `InvalidGroupIdException`               |   | The group id is empty or otherwise invalid                 |
| 25   | UNKNOWN_MEMBER_ID            | `UnknownMemberIdException`              |   | The coordinator does not know this member                  |
| 26   | INVALID_SESSION_TIMEOUT      | `InvalidSessionTimeoutException`        |   | Outside `group.{min,max}.session.timeout.ms` of the broker |
| 27   | REBALANCE_IN_PROGRESS        | `RebalanceInProgressException`          |   | The group is rebalancing, the member has to rejoin         |
| 28   | INVALID_COMMIT_OFFSET_SIZE   | `InvalidCommitOffsetSizeException`      |   | The committed offset entry is too large for the log        |
| 29   | TOPIC_AUTHORIZATION_FAILED   | `TopicAuthorizationFailedException`     |   | Not authorized to access the topic                         |
| 30   | GROUP_AUTHORIZATION_FAILED   | `GroupAuthorizationFailedException`     |   | Not authorized to access the group                         |
| 31   | CLUSTER_AUTHORIZATION_FAILED | `ClusterAuthorizationFailedException`   |   | Not authorized to perform a cluster operation              |
| 32   | INVALID_TIMESTAMP            | `InvalidTimestampException`             |   | The `Timestamp` of a message format v1 record is outside `message.timestamp.difference.max.ms` |
| 33   | UNSUPPORTED_SASL_MECHANISM   | `UnsupportedSaslMechanismException`     |   | The broker does not offer the SASL mechanism the handshake asked for |
| 34   | ILLEGAL_SASL_STATE           | `IllegalSaslStateException`             |   | A SaslHandshake arrived at a moment the connection cannot use it |
| 35   | UNSUPPORTED_VERSION          | `UnsupportedVersionException`           |   | The version of the api is not supported - only ApiVersions ever sends it |
| 36   | TOPIC_ALREADY_EXISTS         | `TopicExistsException`                  |   | A topic of that name already exists (CreateTopics)         |
| 37   | INVALID_PARTITIONS           | `InvalidPartitionsException`            |   | The number of partitions is invalid (CreateTopics)         |
| 38   | INVALID_REPLICATION_FACTOR   | `InvalidReplicationFactorException`     |   | The replication factor is invalid (CreateTopics)           |
| 39   | INVALID_REPLICA_ASSIGNMENT   | `InvalidReplicaAssignmentException`     |   | The manual replica assignment is invalid (CreateTopics)    |
| 40   | INVALID_CONFIG               | `InvalidConfigException`                |   | A topic configuration entry is invalid (CreateTopics)      |
| 41   | NOT_CONTROLLER               | `NotControllerException`                | R | This broker is not the controller of the cluster           |
| 42   | INVALID_REQUEST              | `InvalidRequestException`               |   | The request is malformed; see the broker log               |
| 43   | UNSUPPORTED_FOR_MESSAGE_FORMAT | `UnsupportedForMessageFormatException` |   | The message format version of the broker does not support the request |
| 44   | POLICY_VIOLATION             | `PolicyViolationException`              |   | The request does not satisfy the configured `create.topic.policy.class.name` |
| 45   | OUT_OF_ORDER_SEQUENCE_NUMBER | `OutOfOrderSequenceException`           |   | The broker received an out of order sequence number from an idempotent producer |
| 46   | DUPLICATE_SEQUENCE_NUMBER    | `DuplicateSequenceNumberException`      | R | The broker received a duplicate sequence number — the record is already in the log |
| 47   | INVALID_PRODUCER_EPOCH       | `ProducerFencedException`               |   | An operation with an old epoch: a newer producer took the `transactional.id`, or the transaction expired |
| 48   | INVALID_TXN_STATE            | `InvalidTxnStateException`              |   | A transactional operation in a state the coordinator does not allow it in |
| 49   | INVALID_PRODUCER_ID_MAPPING  | `InvalidPidMappingException`            |   | The producer id is not the one currently assigned to that transactional id |
| 50   | INVALID_TRANSACTION_TIMEOUT  | `InvalidTxnTimeoutException`            |   | The transaction timeout is above `max.transaction.timeout.ms` of the broker |
| 51   | CONCURRENT_TRANSACTIONS      | `ConcurrentTransactionsException`       |   | Another operation on the same transaction is still running |
| 52   | TRANSACTION_COORDINATOR_FENCED | `TransactionCoordinatorFencedException` |   | The coordinator that sent a `WriteTxnMarkers` is not the current one any more |
| 53   | TRANSACTIONAL_ID_AUTHORIZATION_FAILED | `TransactionalIdAuthorizationException` |   | Not authorized to use that transactional id |
| 54   | SECURITY_DISABLED            | `SecurityDisabledException`             |   | The feature is disabled on the broker — what the ACL apis answer without an `authorizer.class.name` |
| 55   | OPERATION_NOT_ATTEMPTED      | `OperationNotAttemptedException`        |   | A batched request failed earlier, so this entry was never tried |

Notes on the mapping:

* **Code 13 changed its meaning between the lines.** It was `StaleLeaderEpochCode` in `ErrorMapping.scala` @ 0.8.2.2
  and no broker ever sent it; from 0.9 it is `NETWORK_EXCEPTION`, whose Java class extends `InvalidMetadataException`
  and is therefore retriable. `NetworkException` on this branch carries that code and is also what the socket layer
  of this client raises for a connection that dies locally - **including the connection a 0.10 broker closes because
  it could not parse a frame**.
* **Code 4 is a leftover.** `Errors.java` skips it with a `// TODO: errorCode 4 for InvalidFetchSize`, but
  `ErrorMapping.scala` still maps `InvalidMessageSizeException` to it and the old Fetch path still produces it, so
  the class stays.
* **41 `NotController` is the only retriable code the 0.10 line adds.** `NotControllerException` extends
  `RetriableException` in `Errors.java` @ 0.10.2.2, because the cure is to look the controller up again and send the
  request to the new one; the other twelve are final. Note that code **40** is `InvalidConfigException` on this
  branch, not the `InvalidConfigurationException` of the Java client, whose name is already taken here by the
  client-side configuration error.
* **35 `UnsupportedVersion` never arrives from an api other than ApiVersions.** Every other key or version a
  0.11.0.3 broker cannot parse closes the connection instead of answering, see "An api the broker does not serve
  closes the connection".
* **46 `DuplicateSequenceNumber` is the only retriable code of the eleven Kafka 0.11 adds**, as in the Java client,
  where `DuplicateSequenceNumberException` extends `RetriableException` in `Errors.java` @ 0.11.0.3 (later releases
  renamed it `DuplicateSequenceException`). A duplicate sequence number means the batch is already in the log, so
  the idempotent producer treats the answer as a success rather than as something to send again; the flag records
  what the Java client says, not what this client does with it. `ConcurrentTransactions` (**51**) is not retriable
  in the hierarchy either, although the transactional producer retries it after a back-off.
* **A 0.11.0.3 broker never sends 46 to a client.** Its duplicate check runs before the sequence validation and
  answers a duplicate of the *last* batch of a producer with the code **0** and the offset of the original append;
  a duplicate of an older batch is **45**, because a 0.11 broker only remembers the last batch of a producer id per
  partition. The retriable flag of 46 therefore describes a code this line implements from the specification, see
  "The idempotent producer".
* **47 is `INVALID_PRODUCER_EPOCH` in `Errors.java` but `ProducerFencedException` in `errors/`**, and this branch
  follows the exception name, as it does everywhere else — the class is what a caller catches.
* **42 `InvalidRequest` is what the broker log calls the frames it closes the socket for**, but it is not sent on
  the wire for them - the connection is already gone. It reaches a client only from an api that has a place to put
  it.
* The Scala sources speak of *consumers* where the Java ones speak of *groups* (`OffsetsLoadInProgressCode`,
  `ConsumerCoordinatorNotAvailableCode`, `NotCoordinatorForConsumerCode`, `UNKNOWN_CONSUMER_ID`,
  `IllegalConsumerGeneration`). The class names of this branch follow `main`, i.e. the group vocabulary.

The codes 29, 30, 31 and 53 need an `authorizer.class.name` on the broker and 44 a `create.topic.policy.class.name`;
the container of `docker-compose.yml` runs without either, so those five are implemented from the specification and
not observed. **54 is observed**, though, and it is the code that says so: an ACL api on a broker without an
authorizer answers `SECURITY_DISABLED` with the message `No Authorizer is configured on the broker`
(`ApiVersionProbeTest::testTheAclApisAreServedButDisabledWithoutAnAuthorizer`).

The codes 56 and above (`KAFKA_STORAGE_ERROR` and everything after it) arrived with Kafka 1.0 and are not on this
branch.

## Wire vectors

Every frame below is a real frame of a real broker. A *request* vector is the frame this client sent and the broker
accepted, a *response* vector is the frame the broker sent back, byte for byte. There are exactly two exceptions,
both marked `source: constructed` in their vector file: `metadata.request.v0.all-topics`, which is built by the
client because a capture of the matching response would carry every topic of the test cluster, and
`offsetfetch.response.v2.group-error`, whose group-level error code the single broker of the container cannot
produce (see the OffsetFetch section).

**A vector is captured on the broker of the line that introduced the api version, and is not re-captured while the
frame does not change.** The api-key table above is what says whether it changed: a version that a 0.11.0.3 broker
still serves with the same schema still has the frame it had, which is why the 120 vectors of the three lines below
travelled up the cascade unchanged and are replayed against the classes of this line. Concretely:

* The version 0 apis were captured on Apache Kafka **0.8.2.2** with the topics `t10-vectors` and `t2-vectors` (three
  partitions each, replication factor 1) and the groups `t10-vectors-group`, `t10-vectors-zk` and
  `t2-vectors-group`.
* What Kafka 0.9 added was captured on the **0.9.0.1** container: Produce v1, Fetch v1, OffsetCommit v2 and
  ControlledShutdown v1, the four apis of the group membership protocol - JoinGroup, SyncGroup, Heartbeat and
  LeaveGroup, with the group `t3-vectors-group` and the client id `t3` - and the group apis DescribeGroups and
  ListGroups, with the topic `t4-vectors`, the group `t4-vectors-group` and one member that had joined with a raw
  JoinGroup and SyncGroup sequence. `controlledshutdown.response.v0` was re-captured there, because its error code
  for an unknown broker id changed from -1 to 8 between the two lines.
* What Kafka 0.10 adds was captured on the **0.10.2.2** container of the `0.10.x` branch, one group of captures per
  api.
  **Message format v1** (`message-format.json`, `kind: structure`): seven message sets read back from the topics
  `t2-mf-v1-none`, `t2-mf-v1-gzip`, `t2-mf-v1-snappy` and `t2-mf-v1-lz4` (the default `message.timestamp.type=CreateTime`)
  and `t2-mf-lat-none`, `t2-mf-lat-gzip` (`message.timestamp.type=LogAppendTime`) with the client id `t2-vectors`,
  the last of them the same lz4 batch fetched with a **Fetch v1** request, which the broker converts down to format
  v0 with the broken KAFKA-3160 checksum. **Metadata v1 and v2**: client id `t10` and the same topic `t10-vectors`
  the version 0 vectors use, plus the internal `__consumer_offsets` of the container (five partitions,
  `offsets.topic.num.partitions=5`). **Produce v2 and Fetch v2/v3**: client id `t4`, the topics `t4-vectors` (three
  partitions) and `t4-vectors-lat` (`message.timestamp.type=LogAppendTime`), which is where the `LogAppendTime` of a
  produce answer comes from. **Offsets v1**: client id `kafka-client-t5-vectors` and the topics `t5-vectors` and
  `t5-vectors-v09` (three partitions each, replication factor 1, the second one created with
  `--config message.format.version=0.9.0`), each holding five messages whose `CreateTime` values run from
  1600000000000 to 1600000004000 in partition 0. **OffsetFetch v2 and JoinGroup v1**: client id `t6`, topic
  `t6-vectors`, groups `t6-vectors-group` and `t6-vectors-join`; the `constructed` response
  `offsetfetch.response.v2.group-error` belongs here, because a one-broker cluster coordinates every group itself
  and therefore never answers a group-level error. **CreateTopics v0/v1 and DeleteTopics v0**: client id and topic
  `t7-vectors`, sent to the controller of the cluster. **SaslHandshake v0**: client id `t8` on the SASL_PLAINTEXT
  listener 9094, with the user `kafkatest` of the `jaas.conf` of that image; the two frames of the PLAIN token
  exchange that follow the handshake are `kind: structure`, because they travel without a request header.
* What Kafka 0.11 adds is captured on the **0.11.0.3** container that `docker-compose.yml` builds
  (`docker/kafka-0.11.0.3/`, four listeners, `log.message.format.version` at its 0.11.0 default), one group of
  captures per api. **ApiVersions** (`api-versions.json`, T1): the request and the answer of **both** versions the
  broker serves - v0 with its 34 api keys and v1 with the same 34 keys plus the trailing `throttle_time_ms` - and
  the answer to a request of the version 7, which is the error code 35 with an empty api array in the version 0
  layout. All five carry the client id `kafka-client-t1-vectors`, each on a connection of its own, because the api
  carries no state; `apiversions.request.v0` and `apiversions.response.v0` were **re-captured** here, because a
  0.11.0.3 broker reports thirteen api keys more than the 0.10.2.2 broker the previous capture came from.

* **The versions that KIP-124 raised** (T3) are captured on the same **0.11.0.3** container, all of them with the
  client id `kafka-client-t3-vectors` on the PLAINTEXT listener: Metadata **v3 and v4**, Offsets **v2** in both
  isolation levels, OffsetCommit **v3**, OffsetFetch **v3**, GroupCoordinator **v1** for a group and for a
  transactional id, JoinGroup **v2**, SyncGroup **v1**, Heartbeat **v1**, DescribeGroups **v1**, ListGroups **v1**,
  LeaveGroup **v1**, CreateTopics **v2** and DeleteTopics **v1**, plus the two frames of **OffsetForLeaderEpoch**
  (`offset-for-leader-epoch.json`, the new file of this group). The topic is `t3-throttle` (three partitions,
  replication factor 1, five messages written with `kafka-console-producer.sh`, two of which landed in partition 0);
  the groups are `t3-throttle-group`, which one member joined, synced, heartbeated and left in that order over one
  connection, and `t3-throttle-offsets`, which committed the offset 3 of `t3-throttle`-0 without being a member of
  anything; the transactional id is `t3-throttle-txn`, and its lookup is what created `__transaction_state` on the
  container. `t3-throttle-created` was created and deleted again for the two topic-admin vectors, and
  `t3-throttle-absent` is the topic that a Metadata v4 with `allow_auto_topic_creation = false` and an
  OffsetForLeaderEpoch both answer for without bringing it into existence. Every throttle time of the group is 0,
  because the container sets no quota.

* What Kafka 0.11 adds to the message format is captured on the **0.11.0.3** container of this line, with the
  client id `t2-vectors` (`message-format.json`, `kind: structure`). The five batches that the Java
  `kafka-console-producer.sh` wrote - plain, gzip, snappy, lz4 into `t2-rb-none`, `t2-rb-gzip`, `t2-rb-snappy`
  and `t2-rb-lz4`, and one into `t2-rb-lat` (`message.timestamp.type=LogAppendTime`) - and the three-batch
  region of `t2-rb-multi` are the **log segments** of those partitions, copied out of the container with
  `docker cp`: the on-disk bytes of a record batch are its wire bytes, which a Fetch v4 of the same partition
  reproduces byte for byte. The batches that no console producer can write - the headers of `t2-rb-headers`,
  the nullable keys and values of `t2-rb-nullable`, and the transactional and control batches of `t2-rb-txn`
  with the transactional id `t2-vectors-txn` - were written with **raw Produce v3** frames and read back with
  **raw Fetch v4** frames (`tests/Fixture/RawRecordBatchProbe.php`), because the classes of those api versions
  belong to a later ticket of the line; the control batch is the real marker that the transaction coordinator
  appended for the raw InitProducerId, AddPartitionsToTxn, Produce, EndTxn sequence, not a constructed one.
  The two `downconverted` vectors are the log of `t2-rb-none` answered to a raw Fetch v3 and a raw Fetch v1.

* **Produce v3 and Fetch v4/v5** are captured on the same **0.11.0.3** container with the client id `t4` and the
  topics `t4-vectors`, `t4-vectors-lat` (`message.timestamp.type=LogAppendTime`) and `t4-vectors-tx`, one partition
  each. The produce vectors write one record batch v2 of two records - the first with a key and two headers - into
  the first two of them; the fetch vectors read `t4-vectors` back with every combination of the two versions and
  the two isolation levels, which is what the `LastStableOffset = -1` / `AbortedTransactions = null` of a
  `read_uncommitted` answer and the empty array of a `read_committed` one come from.
  `fetch.response.v5.aborted` is `t4-vectors-tx` after a transaction that the raw InitProducerId,
  AddPartitionsToTxn, Produce v3, EndTxn(abort) sequence of `tests/Fixture/RawRecordBatchProbe.php` aborted with
  the transactional id `t4-vectors-tx-id`. **Every record of these captures is stamped with the clock of the
  capture, not with a fixed timestamp**: the retention of the broker deletes a segment by the largest timestamp it
  holds, so a batch with a `CreateTime` of 2020 is deleted within minutes on a container whose clock says 2026, and
  the vector would document a log start offset that moves.

* **DeleteRecords, DescribeConfigs and AlterConfigs** are captured on the **0.11.0.3** container as well
  (`delete-records.json`, `describe-configs.json`, `alter-configs.json`, T5), all of them with the client id
  `t5-vectors` on one connection, against the topic `t5-vectors` (one partition, five records) and the broker
  resource `0` of the container. Every api contributes its happy path and two error cases: an offset above the high
  watermark and an unknown topic for DeleteRecords, a broker id the answering broker does not have for
  DescribeConfigs, and an unknown option name and a broker resource for AlterConfigs. The two `describeconfigs`
  requests name their options explicitly, because the answer for `config_names = null` is the ~40 entries of a whole
  topic or the ~180 of a broker.

* **InitProducerId** (`init-producer-id.json`, T7) is captured on the **0.11.0.3** container with the client id
  `t7-vectors` over one connection: the request and the answer for a **null** transactional id, for the
  transactional id `t7-vectors-txn` - whose coordinator was looked up first, which is what creates
  `__transaction_state` - and for a `transaction_timeout_ms` of 999999999, which is the error code 50. The same
  connection produced the four **Produce v3** vectors of the idempotent producer against the topic `t7-vectors`
  (one partition): a batch of two records with the producer id of the first answer, the epoch 0 and the base
  sequence 0, the answer to *the very same frame sent again* - the error code 0 with the base offset of the
  original append - and the request and answer of a batch that starts at the sequence 9, which is the error code
  45. The records carry the clock of the capture, for the reason the Produce v3 and Fetch v4/v5 group gives.

* **The transaction apis 24-28** (`add-partitions-to-txn.json`, `add-offsets-to-txn.json`, `end-txn.json`,
  `write-txn-markers.json`, `txn-offset-commit.json`, T8) are captured on the **0.11.0.3** container with the
  client id `t8-vectors` over one connection, as one real transaction: the transactional id `t8-vectors-txn`
  writes two records into `t8-vectors`-0 (one partition of a three-partition topic), adds the offsets of the group
  `t8-vectors-group`, commits, and is then asked to abort the very transaction it committed, which is the error
  code 48. A second `InitProducerId` for the same id bumps its epoch, and the AddPartitionsToTxn that follows with
  the *old* epoch is the 47. `produce.request.v3.transactional` is the Produce v3 of that transaction, with the
  transactional id in the request and the attributes 0x10 on the batch. The transactional id
  `t8-vectors-abort-txn` writes two records into `t8-vectors-abort`-0 and **aborts** them, which is where
  `endtxn.request.v0.abort`, the `read_committed` answer `fetch.response.v5.aborted-transactions` with its
  non-empty `aborted_transactions` array, and the ABORT control batch `messageformat.v2.control.abort` come from.
  `writetxnmarkers.request.v0` is a real broker-to-broker frame that a client is allowed to send on an unsecured
  cluster: it writes a COMMIT marker for a plain producer id into `t8-vectors-marker`-0. Every record of these
  captures is stamped with the clock of the capture, for the reason the Produce v3 and Fetch v4/v5 group gives.

The last group of vectors is not made of frames at all: the payloads of the consumer group protocol
(`consumer-protocol.json`, `kind: structure`) are the contents of a `member_metadata` or `member_assignment` byte
array, without a `Size` field and without a header, captured on the 0.9.0.1 container with the topic
`t6-consumer-protocol` (three partitions) from groups of Java `kafka-console-consumer.sh` members.

The same bytes live in machine-readable form in [`vectors/`](vectors), one file per api, together with the values
that each frame decodes into. `tests/Compliance` replays all of them through the request and response classes -
decode the frame, compare every field of the scheme, encode the message back and compare the bytes - and
`DocumentationSyncTest` checks that the dumps below and the vector files still hold the same bytes. Neither half can
therefore drift away from the code:

```bash
vendor/bin/phpunit --testsuite compliance
```

Each line of a dump is `<bytes>   # <path of the field> = <value>`; the path is the one of the scheme, e.g.
`topics[t10-vectors].partitions[0].leader`. A line without a comment continues the bytes of the line above it.

### `metadata.request.v0.all-topics`

Metadata request v0 with an empty topic array, which asks for every topic of the cluster.

<!-- vector: metadata.request.v0.all-topics -->
```
00 00 00 12         # messageSize = 18
00 03               # apiKey = 3
00 00               # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 01         # correlationId = 1
00 04 74 65 73 74   # clientId = "test"
00 00 00 00         # topics: 0 item(s)
```

### `metadata.request.v0.single-topic`

Metadata request v0 for one topic; a topic that does not exist yet is created by this request when auto.create.topics.enable is on.

<!-- vector: metadata.request.v0.single-topic -->
```
00 00 00 1e                              # messageSize = 30
00 03                                    # apiKey = 3
00 00                                    # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 07                              # correlationId = 7
00 03 74 31 30                           # clientId = "t10"
00 00 00 01                              # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0b 74 31 30 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[0] = "t10-vectors"
```

### `metadata.response.v0.single-topic`

Metadata of a topic with three partitions on a single-broker cluster.

<!-- vector: metadata.response.v0.single-topic -->
```
00 00 00 80                              # messageSize = 128
00 00 00 07                              # correlationId = 7
00 00 00 01                              # brokers: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                              # brokers[0].nodeId = 0
00 09 31 32 37 2e 30 2e 30 2e 31         # brokers[0].host = "127.0.0.1"
00 00 23 84                              # brokers[0].port = 9092
00 00 00 01                              # topics: 1 item(s)
00 00                                    # topics[t10-vectors].topicErrorCode = 0
00 0b 74 31 30 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t10-vectors].topic = "t10-vectors"
00 00 00 03                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions: 3 item(s)
00 00                                    # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[2].partitionErrorCode = 0
00 00 00 02                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[2].partitionId = 2
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[2].leader = 0
00 00 00 01                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[2].replicas: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[2].replicas[0] = 0
00 00 00 01                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[2].isr: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[2].isr[0] = 0
00 00                                    # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[1].partitionErrorCode = 0
00 00 00 01                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[1].partitionId = 1
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[1].leader = 0
00 00 00 01                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[1].replicas: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[1].replicas[0] = 0
00 00 00 01                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[1].isr: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[1].isr[0] = 0
00 00                                    # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[0].partitionErrorCode = 0
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[0].partitionId = 0
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[0].leader = 0
00 00 00 01                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[0].replicas: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[0].replicas[0] = 0
00 00 00 01                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[0].isr: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                              # topics[t10-vectors].partitions[0].isr[0] = 0
```

### `produce.request.v1`

Produce request v1 with RequiredAcks = 1 and a message set of two uncompressed messages, the second one
without a key. The body is the one of v0, only the api version of the header differs.

<!-- vector: produce.request.v1 -->
```
00 00 00 7f                                       # messageSize = 127
00 00                                             # apiKey = 0
00 01                                             # apiVersion = 1
00 00 00 0b                                       # correlationId = 11
00 02 74 32                                       # clientId = "t2"
00 01                                             # requiredAcks = 1
00 00 05 dc                                       # timeout = 1500
00 00 00 01                                       # topicMessages: 1 item(s)

### `fetch.response.v1.throttled`

A fetch of a client that exceeded `consumer_byte_rate=1024`. The request started at the high watermark, so the
answer carries no record at all - and the broker still delayed it by 5943 ms, because the byte rate of the quota
window is what is over the bound, not this single answer.

<!-- vector: fetch.response.v1.throttled -->

00 00 00 0c                           # correlationId = 12
00 00 17 37                           # throttleTimeMs = 5943

this document, byte for byte. Key 7 (ControlledShutdown) is the only entry whose minimum version is not 0, and the
answer ends with the api array — the `throttle_time_ms` of version 1 is not in this layout.

00 09 00 00 00 03                                 # apiVersions[9] = OffsetFetch, v0-v3
00 0a 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[10] = GroupCoordinator, v0-v1
00 0b 00 00 00 02                                 # apiVersions[11] = JoinGroup, v0-v2
00 0c 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[12] = Heartbeat, v0-v1

00 10 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[16] = ListGroups, v0-v1
00 11 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[17] = SaslHandshake, v0

### `apiversions.request.v1`

ApiVersions request v1, the version this client sends. `API_VERSIONS_REQUEST_V1 = API_VERSIONS_REQUEST_V0` in
`Protocol.java` @ 0.11.0.3, so the frame differs from the one above in the `apiVersion` field of the header and in
nothing else.

<!-- vector: apiversions.request.v1 -->
```
00 00 00 21                                       # messageSize = 33
00 12                                             # apiKey = 18
00 01                                             # apiVersion = 1
00 00 00 66                                       # correlationId = 102
00 17 6b 61 66 6b 61 2d 63 6c 69 65 6e 74 2d 74   # clientId = "kafka-client-t1-vectors"
31 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73
```

### `apiversions.response.v1`

The answer of the same broker to the version 1 request: the identical 34 entries followed by the `throttle_time_ms`
that Kafka 0.11 added (KIP-124). It is the one api of the release that **appends** the field instead of prepending
it, so the first four bytes behind the correlation id are still the error code and the array count — which is what
lets the broker answer an unknown version in the version 0 layout on the same connection.

<!-- vector: apiversions.response.v1 -->
```
00 00 00 da                                       # messageSize = 218
00 00 00 66                                       # correlationId = 102
00 00                                             # errorCode = 0
00 00 00 22                                       # apiVersions: 34 item(s)
00 00 00 00 00 03                                 # apiVersions[0] = Produce, v0-v3
00 01 00 00 00 05                                 # apiVersions[1] = Fetch, v0-v5
00 02 00 00 00 02                                 # apiVersions[2] = Offsets, v0-v2
00 03 00 00 00 04                                 # apiVersions[3] = Metadata, v0-v4
00 04 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[4] = LeaderAndIsr, v0
00 05 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[5] = StopReplica, v0
00 06 00 00 00 03                                 # apiVersions[6] = UpdateMetadata, v0-v3
00 07 00 01 00 01                                 # apiVersions[7] = ControlledShutdown, v1
00 08 00 00 00 03                                 # apiVersions[8] = OffsetCommit, v0-v3
00 09 00 00 00 03                                 # apiVersions[9] = OffsetFetch, v0-v3
00 0a 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[10] = GroupCoordinator, v0-v1
00 0b 00 00 00 02                                 # apiVersions[11] = JoinGroup, v0-v2
00 0c 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[12] = Heartbeat, v0-v1
00 0d 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[13] = LeaveGroup, v0-v1
00 0e 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[14] = SyncGroup, v0-v1
00 0f 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[15] = DescribeGroups, v0-v1
00 10 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[16] = ListGroups, v0-v1
00 11 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[17] = SaslHandshake, v0
00 12 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[18] = ApiVersions, v0-v1
00 13 00 00 00 02                                 # apiVersions[19] = CreateTopics, v0-v2
00 14 00 00 00 01                                 # apiVersions[20] = DeleteTopics, v0-v1
00 15 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[21] = DeleteRecords, v0
00 16 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[22] = InitProducerId, v0

00 18 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[24] = AddPartitionsToTxn, v0
00 19 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[25] = AddOffsetsToTxn, v0
00 1a 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[26] = EndTxn, v0
00 1b 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[27] = WriteTxnMarkers, v0
00 1c 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[28] = TxnOffsetCommit, v0
00 1d 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[29] = DescribeAcls, v0
00 1e 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[30] = CreateAcls, v0
00 1f 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[31] = DeleteAcls, v0
00 20 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[32] = DescribeConfigs, v0
00 21 00 00 00 00                                 # apiVersions[33] = AlterConfigs, v0
00 00 00 00                                       # throttleTimeMs = 0
```

### `apiversions.response.v0.unsupported-version`

The answer to an ApiVersions request that carried the version **7**, which the broker does not serve: the error code
35 (`UnsupportedVersion`) and an empty api array, written in the layout of version 0 — with no throttle time,
although the same broker serves version 1, because `ApiVersionsResponse.unsupportedVersionSend()` hard-codes the
version `0`. Every other key or version a 0.11.0.3 broker cannot parse closes the connection instead; this one is
answered, and the connection stays usable - the very next ApiVersions v1 on the same socket was answered with the
frame above.

<!-- vector: apiversions.response.v0.unsupported-version -->
```
00 00 00 0a                                       # messageSize = 10
00 00 00 65                                       # correlationId = 101
00 23                                             # errorCode = 35 (UnsupportedVersion)
00 00 00 00                                       # apiVersions: 0 item(s)
```

### `saslhandshake.request.v0`

SaslHandshake request v0 selecting the PLAIN mechanism. It is the very first frame of a connection to a SASL listener; only `ApiVersions` may precede it.

<!-- vector: saslhandshake.request.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 13               # messageSize = 19
00 11                     # apiKey = 17
00 00                     # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 11               # correlationId = 17
00 02 74 38               # clientId = "t8"
00 05 50 4c 41 49 4e      # mechanism = "PLAIN"
```

### `saslhandshake.response.v0`

The answer of a broker that accepted the mechanism: error code 0 and its `sasl.enabled.mechanisms`.

<!-- vector: saslhandshake.response.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 11               # messageSize = 17
00 00 00 11               # correlationId = 17
00 00                     # errorCode = 0
00 00 00 01               # enabledMechanisms: 1 item(s)
00 05 50 4c 41 49 4e      # enabledMechanisms[0] = "PLAIN"
```

### `saslplain.token.client`

The initial response of the PLAIN mechanism for the user `kafkatest`, `authzid \0 authcid \0 passwd` (RFC 4616). The frame has no request header at all - the length prefix is the whole framing, and the `SaslAuthenticate` request that would wrap it is Kafka 1.0.

<!-- vector: saslplain.token.client -->
```
00 00 00 1b                                       # token: 27 bytes
00                                                # authorization id = "" (empty)
6b 61 66 6b 61 74 65 73 74                        # user name = "kafkatest"
00
6b 61 66 6b 61 74 65 73 74 2d 73 65 63 72 65 74   # password = "kafkatest-secret"
```

### `saslplain.token.server`

The answer of a completed PLAIN exchange: the empty token of `PlainSaslServer.evaluateResponse()`, i.e. a frame that is nothing but a zero length. Ordinary requests follow it on the same connection.

<!-- vector: saslplain.token.server -->
```
00 00 00 00   # token: 0 bytes
```

### `saslhandshake.request.v0.unsupported-mechanism`

SaslHandshake request v0 asking for GSSAPI on a broker whose `sasl.enabled.mechanisms` is `PLAIN`.

<!-- vector: saslhandshake.request.v0.unsupported-mechanism -->
```
00 00 00 14                  # messageSize = 20
00 11                        # apiKey = 17
00 00                        # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 12                  # correlationId = 18
00 02 74 38                  # clientId = "t8"
00 06 47 53 53 41 50 49      # mechanism = "GSSAPI"
```

### `saslhandshake.response.v0.unsupported-mechanism`

Error code 33 (UnsupportedSaslMechanism) together with the mechanisms the broker does have. The broker closes the connection right after this frame, so there is no second chance on it.

<!-- vector: saslhandshake.response.v0.unsupported-mechanism -->
```
00 00 00 11               # messageSize = 17
00 00 00 12               # correlationId = 18
00 21                     # errorCode = 33 (UnsupportedSaslMechanism)
00 00 00 01               # enabledMechanisms: 1 item(s)
00 05 50 4c 41 49 4e      # enabledMechanisms[0] = "PLAIN"
```

### `createtopics.request.v1`

CreateTopics request v1 that creates one topic with two partitions, one replica per partition, no explicit replica assignment and one topic level option, waiting up to 30 seconds for the controller.

<!-- vector: createtopics.request.v1 -->
```
00 00 00 4e                                 # messageSize = 78
00 13                                       # apiKey = 19
00 01                                       # apiVersion = 1
00 00 00 64                                 # correlationId = 100
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73         # clientId = "t7-vectors"
00 00 00 01                                 # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73         # topics[t7-vectors].topic = "t7-vectors"
00 00 00 02                                 # topics[t7-vectors].numPartitions = 2
00 01                                       # topics[t7-vectors].replicationFactor = 1
00 00 00 00                                 # topics[t7-vectors].replicaAssignment: 0 item(s)
00 00 00 01                                 # topics[t7-vectors].configs: 1 item(s)
00 0c 72 65 74 65 6e 74 69 6f 6e 2e 6d 73   # topics[t7-vectors].configs[retention.ms].configKey = "retention.ms"
00 07 33 36 30 30 30 30 30                  # topics[t7-vectors].configs[retention.ms].configValue = "3600000"
00 00 75 30                                 # timeout = 30000
00                                          # validateOnly = false
```

### `createtopics.response.v1`

CreateTopics response v1 of the controller for a topic it created: error code 0 and a null error message (ff ff), the null of a NULLABLE_STRING.

<!-- vector: createtopics.response.v1 -->
```
00 00 00 18                           # messageSize = 24
00 00 00 64                           # correlationId = 100
00 00 00 01                           # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t7-vectors].topic = "t7-vectors"
00 00                                 # topics[t7-vectors].errorCode = 0
ff ff                                 # topics[t7-vectors].errorMessage = null
```

### `createtopics.response.v1.topic-exists`

CreateTopics response v1 for a topic that already exists: the error code 36 (TopicAlreadyExists) and the message of the exception the controller caught, which only version 1 of the api carries.

<!-- vector: createtopics.response.v1.topic-exists -->
```
00 00 00 3a                                       # messageSize = 58
00 00 00 65                                       # correlationId = 101
00 00 00 01                                       # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # topics[t7-vectors].topic = "t7-vectors"
00 24                                             # topics[t7-vectors].errorCode = 36
00 22 54 6f 70 69 63 20 27 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74   # topics[t7-vectors].errorMessage = "Topic 't7-vectors' already exists."
6f 72 73 27 20 61 6c 72 65 61 64 79 20 65 78 69
73 74 73 2e
```

CreateTopics request v0, the version without the trailing validate_only flag, creating a topic with one partition, one replica and no topic level options.

00 13                                 # apiKey = 19
00 00                                 # apiVersion = 0

00 01                                 # topics[t7-vectors].replicationFactor = 1
00 00 00 00                           # topics[t7-vectors].replicaAssignment: 0 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topics[t7-vectors].configs: 0 item(s)

CreateTopics response v0 of the controller for a topic it created: the bare topic and error code, without the error message of version 1.

### `messageformat.v1.createtime.uncompressed`

Three uncompressed records of message format v1 on a topic with the default message.timestamp.type=CreateTime, read back with a Fetch v2 request. Every message carries its own timestamp and the absolute offset the broker assigned to it.

<!-- vector: messageformat.v1.createtime.uncompressed -->
```
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # [0].offset = 0
00 00 00 1b                                       # [0].messageSize = 27

### `messageformat.v0.lz4.broken-checksum`

The lz4 batch above, read with a Fetch v1 request: the broker converted the stored message format v1 down to v0, which drops the timestamps, turns the inner offsets into absolute ones (3, 4, 5) and writes the LZ4 frame with the broken descriptor checksum of KAFKA-3160 (04 22 4d 18 60 40 1a).

<!-- vector: messageformat.v0.lz4.broken-checksum -->

d4 a6 5e 7e                                       # [0].message.crc = 3567672958
00                                                # [0].message.magicByte = 0
03                                                # [0].message.attributes = 0x03 (codec lz4)
ff ff ff ff                                       # [0].message.key = null
00 00 00 62                                       # [0].message.value: 98 bytes (the lz4 stream of the inner message set)
04 22 4d 18 60 40 1a 53 00 00 00 12 00 01 00
f2 09 03 00 00 00 13 61 57 e5 5e 00 00 ff ff
ff ff 00 00 00 05 61 6c 70 68 61 1e 00 a1 00
04 00 00 00 16 37 ba 21 8a 10 00 40 03 6b 65
79 22 00 51 62 72 61 76 6f 12 00 b5 00 00 05
00 00 00 15 18 b1 9e 64 41 00 80 07 63 68 61
72 6c 69 65 00 00 00 00
```

00 00 00 01                                       # topicMessages[t4-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topicMessages[t4-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00 00 61 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 25   # topicMessages[t4-vectors].partitions[0].messageSet = 97 bytes
53 1f 2a 06 01 00 00 00 01 5a c2 ac f8 00 00 00
00 01 31 00 00 00 0e 76 65 63 74 6f 72 2d 76 61
6c 75 65 2d 31 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 01 00 00 00

ff ff ff 00 00 00 0e 76 65 63 74 6f 72 2d 76 61
6c 75 65 2d 32

### `produce.response.v2`

Produce response v2 of a topic that keeps the CreateTime of the producer: the base offset the message set was
appended at, the LogAppendTime that version 2 added behind it - -1, "the broker stamped nothing" - and the
ThrottleTime of version 1, which closes the answer.

<!-- vector: produce.response.v2 -->
```
00 00 00 32                           # messageSize = 50
00 00 00 15                           # correlationId = 21
00 00 00 01                           # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 34 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t4-vectors].topic = "t4-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # topics[t4-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00                                 # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00               # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].baseOffset = 0
ff ff ff ff ff ff ff ff               # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].logAppendTime = -1
00 00 00 00                           # throttleTime = 0
```

### `produce.response.v2.log-append-time`

The answer of the same request for a topic created with message.timestamp.type=LogAppendTime: the broker overwrote
the timestamp of every message of the batch with its own clock and reports that value as the LogAppendTime of the
partition, 1788933842981 = 2026-09-09T06:04:02.981Z.

```

00 00 00 16                                       # correlationId = 22
00 00 00 01                                       # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0e 74 34 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 6c 61 74   # topics[t4-vectors-lat].topic = "t4-vectors-lat"
00 00 00 01                                       # topics[t4-vectors-lat].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topics[t4-vectors-lat].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00                                             # topics[t4-vectors-lat].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # topics[t4-vectors-lat].partitions[0].baseOffset = 0
00 00 01 a0 84 c4 28 25                           # topics[t4-vectors-lat].partitions[0].logAppendTime = 1788933842981
00 00 00 00                                       # throttleTime = 0
```

### `fetch.request.v2`

Fetch request v2 of an ordinary consumer (ReplicaId = -1) for one partition, with a MaxBytes of 4096 for it. The
frame is the one of version 1; the version alone tells the broker that the client understands message format v1, so
the log is not converted down to format v0.

<!-- vector: fetch.request.v2 -->
```
00 00 00 3c                           # messageSize = 60
00 01                                 # apiKey = 1
00 02                                 # apiVersion = 2
00 00 00 17                           # correlationId = 23
00 02 74 34                           # clientId = "t4"
ff ff ff ff                           # replicaId = -1
00 00 00 64                           # maxWaitTime = 100
00 00 00 01                           # minBytes = 1
00 00 00 01                           # topicPartitions: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 34 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].topic = "t4-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00               # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[0].fetchOffset = 0
00 00 10 00                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[0].maxBytes = 4096
```

producer stamped on them.

00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02                           # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].highWaterMarkOffset = 2
00 00 00 61 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 25   # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].messageSet = 97 bytes
53 1f 2a 06 01 00 00 00 01 5a c2 ac f8 00 00 00
00 01 31 00 00 00 0e 76 65 63 74 6f 72 2d 76 61
6c 75 65 2d 31 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 01 00 00 00
24 3c 92 3d 14 01 00 00 00 01 5a c2 ac f8 01 ff
ff ff ff 00 00 00 0e 76 65 63 74 6f 72 2d 76 61
6c 75 65 2d 32

### `fetch.request.v3.max-bytes`

Fetch request v3 for two partitions of one topic with a request-level MaxBytes of 40 bytes, which is less than a
single message of either of them. The order of the partitions in this frame is the order in which the broker fills
the answer.

<!-- vector: fetch.request.v3.max-bytes -->
```

00 01                                 # apiKey = 1
00 03                                 # apiVersion = 3
00 00 00 1b                           # correlationId = 27
00 02 74 34                           # clientId = "t4"
ff ff ff ff                           # replicaId = -1
00 00 00 64                           # maxWaitTime = 100
00 00 00 01                           # minBytes = 1
00 00 00 28                           # maxBytes = 40
00 00 00 01                           # topicPartitions: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 34 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].topic = "t4-vectors"
00 00 00 02                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions: 2 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02               # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[0].fetchOffset = 2
00 00 10 00                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[0].maxBytes = 4096
00 00 00 01                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[1].partition = 1
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00               # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[1].fetchOffset = 0
00 00 10 00                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[1].maxBytes = 4096
```

### `fetch.response.v3.max-bytes`

What the broker answers to it: the first partition ignores the budget and returns its 52-byte message in full, so
that the consumer can make progress, and the second one - whose turn comes when the 40 bytes are already used up -
comes back with an empty message set although its high water mark shows that there is a message to read.

<!-- vector: fetch.response.v3.max-bytes -->

00 00 00 00                                       # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00 00 01                                       # topics: 1 item(s)

00 00 00 02                                       # topics[t4-vectors].partitions: 2 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00                                             # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 03                           # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].highWaterMarkOffset = 3
00 00 00 34 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02 00 00 00 28   # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].messageSet = 52 bytes
0f fd 22 d1 01 00 00 00 01 5a c2 ac f8 00 ff ff
ff ff 00 00 00 12 62 75 64 67 65 74 2d 70 61 72
74 69 74 69 6f 6e 2d 30
00 00 00 01                                       # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[1].partition = 1
00 00                                             # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[1].errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 01                           # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[1].highWaterMarkOffset = 1
00 00 00 00                                       # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[1].messageSet = 0 bytes
```

00 00 00 1f                                       # correlationId = 31

75 70

00 00 00 01                                       # topicPartitions[t6-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topicPartitions[t6-vectors].partitions[0] = 0
```

OffsetFetch answer v2 for the committed partition: the same topics array the lower versions answer, and the

<!-- vector: offsetfetch.response.v2 -->

00 00 00 2c                           # messageSize = 44
00 00 00 1f                           # correlationId = 31
00 00 00 01                           # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 36 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t6-vectors].topic = "t6-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # topics[t6-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)

00 00 00 00 00 00 00 01               # topics[t6-vectors].partitions[0].offset = 1
00 02 74 36                           # topics[t6-vectors].partitions[0].metadata = "t6"
00 00                                 # topics[t6-vectors].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
00 00                                 # errorCode = 0

### `offsetfetch.request.v2.all-topics`

OffsetFetch request v2 with a NULL topic array (ff ff ff ff), which asks the coordinator for every topic-partition
the group has a committed offset for. Version 1 closes the connection on the same four bytes.

<!-- vector: offsetfetch.request.v2.all-topics -->
```
00 00 00 22                                       # messageSize = 34
00 09                                             # apiKey = 9
00 02                                             # apiVersion = 2
00 00 00 20                                       # correlationId = 32
00 02 74 36                                       # clientId = "t6"
00 10 74 36 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 67 72 6f   # consumerGroup = "t6-vectors-group"
75 70
ff ff ff ff                                       # topicPartitions: null
```

The answer to the null topic array: every committed topic-partition of the group, byte for byte the answer of the
explicit request above.

<!-- vector: offsetfetch.response.v2.all-topics -->
```
00 00 00 2c                           # messageSize = 44
00 00 00 20                           # correlationId = 32
00 00 00 01                           # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 36 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t6-vectors].topic = "t6-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # topics[t6-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topics[t6-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0

00 02 74 36                           # topics[t6-vectors].partitions[0].metadata = "t6"
00 00                                 # topics[t6-vectors].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
00 00                                 # errorCode = 0

### `offsetfetch.request.v2.no-topics`

```
00 00 00 22                                       # messageSize = 34
00 09                                             # apiKey = 9
00 02                                             # apiVersion = 2
00 00 00 21                                       # correlationId = 33

00 10 74 36 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 67 72 6f   # consumerGroup = "t6-vectors-group"
75 70
00 00 00 00                                       # topicPartitions: 0 item(s)
```

### `offsetfetch.response.v2.no-topics`

The answer to the empty topic array of the same group: no topic and the error code 0, although the group has a
committed offset.

<!-- vector: offsetfetch.response.v2.no-topics -->
```

00 00 00 21   # correlationId = 33
00 00 00 00   # topics: 0 item(s)
00 00         # errorCode = 0
```

### `offsetfetch.response.v2.no-committed-offset`

The answer for a partition without a committed offset: offset -1, EMPTY metadata (not null) and the error code 0,
plus the group-level 0. Version 0 reports the error code 3 for the same case.

<!-- vector: offsetfetch.response.v2.no-committed-offset -->
