00 00                                 # errorCode = 0
```

### `offsetfetch.response.v2.group-error`

The shape of a group-level error of v2: an EMPTY topics array and the error code 16 (NotCoordinatorForGroup) after
it, as OffsetFetchRequest.getErrorResponse() @ 0.10.2.2 builds it. Constructed, because the single-broker container
coordinates every group itself and therefore never answers 14, 15, 16 or 30 - see the 'Broker quirks' note in the
OffsetFetch section.

<!-- vector: offsetfetch.response.v2.group-error -->
```
00 00 00 0a   # messageSize = 10
00 00 00 23   # correlationId = 35
00 00 00 00   # topics: 0 item(s)
00 10         # errorCode = 16
```

### `joingroup.request.v1`

JoinGroup request v1 of a client that has no member id yet: the rebalance_timeout of Kafka 0.10.1 (30000) sits
between the session timeout (10000) and the member id, and it does not have to lie inside the
group.min/max.session.timeout.ms of the broker.

<!-- vector: joingroup.request.v1 -->
```
00 00 00 56                                       # messageSize = 86
00 0b                                             # apiKey = 11
00 01                                             # apiVersion = 1
00 00 00 28                                       # correlationId = 40
00 02 74 36                                       # clientId = "t6"
00 0f 74 36 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 6a 6f 69   # consumerGroup = "t6-vectors-join"
6e
00 00 27 10                                       # sessionTimeout = 10000
00 00 75 30                                       # rebalanceTimeout = 30000
00 00                                             # memberId = ""
00 08 63 6f 6e 73 75 6d 65 72                     # protocolType = "consumer"
00 00 00 01                                       # groupProtocols: 1 item(s)
00 05 72 61 6e 67 65                              # groupProtocols[range].name = "range"
00 00 00 16 00 00 00 00 00 01 00 0a 74 36 2d 76   # groupProtocols[range].metadata = 22 bytes
65 63 74 6f 72 73 ff ff ff ff
```

JOIN_GROUP_RESPONSE_V0 in Protocol.java @ 0.10.2.2 - for the first and only member of a fresh group, which is its
leader and therefore receives the metadata of every member.

### `messageformat.v2.none.transactional`

The data batch of a committed transaction: bit 4 of the attributes is set, the producer id and the epoch are the ones InitProducerId handed out and baseSequence is 0. Written with the raw sequence InitProducerId, AddPartitionsToTxn, Produce v3 with a transactional id, EndTxn.

<!-- vector: messageformat.v2.none.transactional -->

00 00 01 74 87 6e 80 05            # batches[0].maxTimestamp = 1600000000005
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02            # batches[0].producerId = 2
00 01                              # batches[0].producerEpoch = 1
00 00 00 00                        # batches[0].baseSequence = 0 (last sequence 1)
00 00 00 02                        # batches[0].recordCount = 2
22                                 # batches[0].records[0].length = 17
00                                 # batches[0].records[0].attributes = 0
00                                 # batches[0].records[0].timestampDelta = 0 (timestamp 1600000000000)
00                                 # batches[0].records[0].offsetDelta = 0 (offset 0)

00                                 # batches[0].records[1].headers: 0 item(s)
```

### `messageformat.v2.none.control`

The control batch that the transaction coordinator appended when the transaction above was committed: the attributes are 0x30 (transactional and control), the batch holds one record whose key is the version 0 and the type 1 (COMMIT) and whose value is the version 0 and the coordinator epoch 0, and baseSequence is -1. A client never hands such a record to an application.

<!-- vector: messageformat.v2.none.control -->
```
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02   # batches[0].baseOffset = 2
00 00 00 42               # batches[0].batchLength = 66
00 00 00 00               # batches[0].partitionLeaderEpoch = 0
02                        # batches[0].magic = 2
b8 ef 36 37               # batches[0].crc = 3102684727 (CRC-32C of everything from attributes on)
00 30                     # batches[0].attributes = 0x0030 (codec none, CreateTime, transactional, control)
00 00 00 00               # batches[0].lastOffsetDelta = 0 (last offset 2)
00 00 01 a0 85 c3 dc 9c   # batches[0].firstTimestamp = 1788950600860
00 00 01 a0 85 c3 dc 9c   # batches[0].maxTimestamp = 1788950600860
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02   # batches[0].producerId = 2
00 01                     # batches[0].producerEpoch = 1
ff ff ff ff               # batches[0].baseSequence = -1 (last sequence -1)
00 00 00 01               # batches[0].recordCount = 1
20                        # batches[0].records[0].length = 16
00                        # batches[0].records[0].attributes = 0
00                        # batches[0].records[0].timestampDelta = 0 (timestamp 1788950600860)
00                        # batches[0].records[0].offsetDelta = 0 (offset 2)
08                        # batches[0].records[0].key: 4 bytes
00 00                     # the control record key: version = 0
00 01                     # type = 1 (COMMIT)
0c                        # batches[0].records[0].value: 6 bytes
00 00                     # the end transaction marker: version = 0
00 00 00 00               # coordinatorEpoch = 0
00                        # batches[0].records[0].headers: 0 item(s)
```

The log of t2-rb-none - the record batch v2 of messageformat.v2.none.createtime - answered to a Fetch v3 request, which a client that does not understand the message format v2 sends. The broker converts the batch into three uncompressed messages of the message format v1, keeping the offsets and the timestamps.

ff ff ff ff               # [0].message.key = null
00 00 00 05               # [0].message.value: 5 bytes
61 6c 70 68 61            # "alpha"
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 01   # [1].offset = 1
00 00 00 1e               # [1].messageSize = 30
dd b5 15 b5               # [1].message.crc = 3719632309
01                        # [1].message.magicByte = 1
00                        # [1].message.attributes = 0x00 (codec none, CreateTime)
00 00 01 a0 85 be e2 a2   # [1].message.timestamp = 1788950274722
00 00 00 03               # [1].message.key: 3 bytes
6b 65 79                  # "key"
00 00 00 05               # [1].message.value: 5 bytes
62 72 61 76 6f            # "bravo"
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02   # [2].offset = 2
00 00 00 1d               # [2].messageSize = 29
c2 c2 98 e0               # [2].message.crc = 3267533024
01                        # [2].message.magicByte = 1
00                        # [2].message.attributes = 0x00 (codec none, CreateTime)
00 00 01 a0 85 be e2 a2   # [2].message.timestamp = 1788950274722
ff ff ff ff               # [2].message.key = null

63 68 61 72 6c 69 65      # "charlie"

### `produce.request.v3`

Produce request v3 with RequiredAcks = 1, the null TransactionalId of a producer that is not in a transaction, and a
record batch of the message format v2 with two records - the first of them with a key and two headers, the second
without either. Version 3 is the only version that can carry any of the three.

### `produce.response.v3`

Produce response v3 of a topic that keeps the CreateTime of the producer. The frame is the one of version 2, byte for
byte - PRODUCE_RESPONSE_V3 is PRODUCE_RESPONSE_V2 in Protocol.java @ 0.11.0.3 - so it carries the base offset the
batch was appended at, the LogAppendTime -1 of a CreateTime topic and the ThrottleTime of version 1, and no
log_start_offset: that field is Kafka 1.0.

<!-- vector: produce.response.v3 -->
```
00 00 00 32                           # messageSize = 50
00 00 00 15                           # correlationId = 21
00 00 00 01                           # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 34 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t4-vectors].topic = "t4-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # topics[t4-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00                                 # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00               # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].baseOffset = 0
ff ff ff ff ff ff ff ff               # topics[t4-vectors].partitions[0].logAppendTime = -1
00 00 00 00                           # throttleTime = 0
```

### `fetch.request.v4`

Fetch request v4, which adds the IsolationLevel byte behind the request-level MaxBytes of version 3 - 0,
read_uncommitted. Its partition entries are the ones of version 3: the LogStartOffset only arrived in version 5.

```

00 01                                 # apiKey = 1
00 04                                 # apiVersion = 4

ff ff ff ff                           # replicaId = -1
00 00 00 64                           # maxWaitTime = 100

00 00 10 00                           # topicPartitions[t4-vectors].partitions[0].maxBytes = 4096
```

entry. A read_uncommitted fetch is answered with the LastStableOffset -1 and the element count -1 of a null
AbortedTransactions array - the broker computes neither of them for a consumer that does not care about transactions.

### `fetch.response.v5.aborted`

A read_committed answer of a partition whose only transaction was aborted: one entry in AbortedTransactions with the
producer id of that transaction and the first offset it wrote. The record set still holds both batches - the
transactional data batch at the offset 0 and the control batch with the ABORT marker that the coordinator wrote at the
offset 1 - because the broker does not filter anything: dropping the records of an aborted producer is the job of the
consumer.

<!-- vector: fetch.response.v5.aborted -->

00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # topics[t4-vectors-tx].partitions[0].abortedTransactions[0].firstOffset = 0

### `initproducerid.request.v0`

InitProducerId request v0 of an idempotent producer: a null transactional id, which any broker answers with a
producer id of its own block, and the `transaction.timeout.ms` default of 60000 ms, which it ignores for a null id.

<!-- vector: initproducerid.request.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 1a                           # messageSize = 26
00 16                                 # apiKey = 22
00 00                                 # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 01                           # correlationId = 1
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # clientId = "t7-vectors"
ff ff                                 # transactionalId = null
00 00 ea 60                           # transactionTimeoutMs = 60000
```

### `initproducerid.response.v0`

The answer for a null transactional id: the next producer id of the block the broker reserved in ZooKeeper, always
with the epoch 0. Every request answers a new id.

<!-- vector: initproducerid.response.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 14               # messageSize = 20
00 00 00 01               # correlationId = 1
00 00 00 00               # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00                     # errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 0b   # producerId = 11
00 00                     # producerEpoch = 0
```

### `initproducerid.request.v0.transactional`

InitProducerId request v0 of a transactional producer: only the transaction coordinator of `t7-vectors-txn` may
answer it, and the transaction timeout is the one the coordinator holds against `transaction.max.timeout.ms`.

<!-- vector: initproducerid.request.v0.transactional -->
```
00 00 00 28                                       # messageSize = 40
00 16                                             # apiKey = 22
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 03                                       # correlationId = 3
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t7-vectors"
00 0e 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t7-vectors-txn"
00 00 ea 60                                       # transactionTimeoutMs = 60000
```

### `initproducerid.response.v0.transactional`

The answer of the transaction coordinator: the producer id that `__transaction_state` holds for the id, with the
epoch 0 of its very first producer. Every later request for the same id answers the same producer id with an epoch
one higher, which fences the producer before it.

<!-- vector: initproducerid.response.v0.transactional -->
```
00 00 00 14               # messageSize = 20
00 00 00 03               # correlationId = 3
00 00 00 00               # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00                     # errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 0c   # producerId = 12
00 00                     # producerEpoch = 0
```

### `initproducerid.request.v0.invalid-timeout`

InitProducerId request v0 with a transaction timeout of 999999999 ms, above the `transaction.max.timeout.ms` of the
broker (900000 by default). The same value next to a null transactional id is accepted.

<!-- vector: initproducerid.request.v0.invalid-timeout -->
```
00 00 00 28                                       # messageSize = 40
00 16                                             # apiKey = 22
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 04                                       # correlationId = 4
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t7-vectors"
00 0e 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t7-vectors-txn"
3b 9a c9 ff                                       # transactionTimeoutMs = 999999999
```

### `initproducerid.response.v0.invalid-timeout`

The answer to a timeout the broker will not accept: the error code 50 (InvalidTransactionTimeout) and -1 as the
producer id and as the epoch, which is what every answer with an error carries.

<!-- vector: initproducerid.response.v0.invalid-timeout -->
```
00 00 00 14               # messageSize = 20
00 00 00 04               # correlationId = 4
00 00 00 00               # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 32                     # errorCode = 50
ff ff ff ff ff ff ff ff   # producerId = -1
ff ff                     # producerEpoch = -1
```

### `produce.request.v3.idempotent`

Produce v3 of an idempotent producer: the record batch v2 carries the producer id the broker handed out (11), the
epoch 0 and the base sequence 0 of its two records, and `RequiredAcks` is -1, which the guarantee requires. The
transactional id of the request stays null - the batch is idempotent, not transactional.

<!-- vector: produce.request.v3.idempotent -->
```
00 00 00 9f                                       # messageSize = 159
00 00                                             # apiKey = 0
00 03                                             # apiVersion = 3
00 00 00 05                                       # correlationId = 5
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t7-vectors"
ff ff                                             # transactionalId = null
ff ff                                             # requiredAcks = -1
00 00 13 88                                       # timeout = 5000
00 00 00 01                                       # topicMessages: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # topicMessages[t7-vectors].topic = "t7-vectors"
00 00 00 01                                       # topicMessages[t7-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topicMessages[t7-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00 00 67 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 5b   # topicMessages[t7-vectors].partitions[0].messageSet = 103 bytes
ff ff ff ff 02 0f a4 4a 07 00 00 00 00 00 01 00
00 01 a0 86 49 bf dc 00 00 01 a0 86 49 bf dc 00
00 00 00 00 00 00 0b 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00
02 28 00 00 00 01 1c 69 64 65 6d 70 6f 74 65 6e
74 20 6f 6e 65 00 28 00 00 02 01 1c 69 64 65 6d
70 6f 74 65 6e 74 20 74 77 6f 00
```

### `produce.response.v3.duplicate`

The answer to the very same frame sent a second time, the retry of a batch whose acknowledgement got lost: the
broker recognises the triple (producer id, epoch, sequence range) of the batch it appended last and answers the
error code 0 with the offset of that **original** append, without writing anything. The `LogAppendTime` gives it
away - the topic keeps the `CreateTime` of the producer, so a real append answers -1 there, while a duplicate is
answered with the timestamp the log holds for the batch.

<!-- vector: produce.response.v3.duplicate -->
```
00 00 00 32                           # messageSize = 50
00 00 00 06                           # correlationId = 6
00 00 00 01                           # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t7-vectors].topic = "t7-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # topics[t7-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topics[t7-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00                                 # topics[t7-vectors].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00               # topics[t7-vectors].partitions[0].baseOffset = 0
00 00 01 a0 86 49 bf dc               # topics[t7-vectors].partitions[0].logAppendTime = 1788959375324
00 00 00 00                           # throttleTime = 0
```

### `produce.request.v3.out-of-order-sequence`

Produce v3 whose record batch starts at the sequence 9 while the broker expects 2 for this producer id and
partition: the gap that an idempotent producer must never leave.

<!-- vector: produce.request.v3.out-of-order-sequence -->
```
00 00 00 88                                       # messageSize = 136
00 00                                             # apiKey = 0
00 03                                             # apiVersion = 3
00 00 00 07                                       # correlationId = 7
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t7-vectors"
ff ff                                             # transactionalId = null
ff ff                                             # requiredAcks = -1
00 00 13 88                                       # timeout = 5000
00 00 00 01                                       # topicMessages: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # topicMessages[t7-vectors].topic = "t7-vectors"
00 00 00 01                                       # topicMessages[t7-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topicMessages[t7-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00 00 50 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 44   # topicMessages[t7-vectors].partitions[0].messageSet = 80 bytes
ff ff ff ff 02 25 98 6c e6 00 00 00 00 00 00 00
00 01 a0 86 49 bf dc 00 00 01 a0 86 49 bf dc 00
00 00 00 00 00 00 0b 00 00 00 00 00 09 00 00 00
01 24 00 00 00 01 18 6f 75 74 20 6f 66 20 6f 72
64 65 72 00
```

### `produce.response.v3.out-of-order-sequence`

The answer to a sequence with a gap in it: the error code 45 (OutOfOrderSequenceNumber), no offset and no
`LogAppendTime`. The same code answers a duplicate of a batch that is not the last one the broker appended.

<!-- vector: produce.response.v3.out-of-order-sequence -->
```
00 00 00 32                           # messageSize = 50
00 00 00 07                           # correlationId = 7
00 00 00 01                           # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 37 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t7-vectors].topic = "t7-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # topics[t7-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topics[t7-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 2d                                 # topics[t7-vectors].partitions[0].errorCode = 45
ff ff ff ff ff ff ff ff               # topics[t7-vectors].partitions[0].baseOffset = -1
ff ff ff ff ff ff ff ff               # topics[t7-vectors].partitions[0].logAppendTime = -1
00 00 00 00                           # throttleTime = 0
```

### `addpartitionstotxn.request.v0`

AddPartitionsToTxn v0: `t8-vectors`-0 is enrolled into the transaction of `t8-vectors-txn`, with the
producer id and the epoch the coordinator handed out. This is the first request of a transaction and the
one that starts it on the broker.

<!-- vector: addpartitionstotxn.request.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 46                                       # messageSize = 70
00 18                                             # apiKey = 24
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 b7                                       # correlationId = 183
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 0e 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t8-vectors-txn"
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # producerId = 0
00 02                                             # producerEpoch = 2
00 00 00 01                                       # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # topics[t8-vectors].topic = "t8-vectors"
00 00 00 01                                       # topics[t8-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topics[t8-vectors].partitions[0] = 0
```

### `addpartitionstotxn.response.v0`

The answer of the transaction coordinator: one entry per requested partition, the error code 0 for a
partition that is now part of the transaction. There is no top-level error code, and the array is called
`errors` although it also carries the partitions that succeeded.

<!-- vector: addpartitionstotxn.response.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 22                           # messageSize = 34
00 00 00 b7                           # correlationId = 183
00 00 00 00                           # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00 00 01                           # errors: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # errors[t8-vectors].topic = "t8-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # errors[t8-vectors].partitionErrors: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # errors[t8-vectors].partitionErrors[0].partition = 0
00 00                                 # errors[t8-vectors].partitionErrors[0].errorCode = 0
```

### `addpartitionstotxn.request.v0.fenced`

The very same request after a second `InitProducerId` for `t8-vectors-txn` bumped its epoch from 2 to 3:
this producer still holds the epoch 2 and is the fenced one.

<!-- vector: addpartitionstotxn.request.v0.fenced -->
```
00 00 00 46                                       # messageSize = 70
00 18                                             # apiKey = 24
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 c1                                       # correlationId = 193
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 0e 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t8-vectors-txn"
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # producerId = 0
00 02                                             # producerEpoch = 2
00 00 00 01                                       # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # topics[t8-vectors].topic = "t8-vectors"
00 00 00 01                                       # topics[t8-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topics[t8-vectors].partitions[0] = 0
```

### `addpartitionstotxn.response.v0.fenced`

The answer to a fenced producer: the error code 47 (InvalidProducerEpoch), reported per partition like
every other error of this api although it is a statement about the transactional id.

<!-- vector: addpartitionstotxn.response.v0.fenced -->
```
00 00 00 22                           # messageSize = 34
00 00 00 c1                           # correlationId = 193
00 00 00 00                           # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00 00 01                           # errors: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # errors[t8-vectors].topic = "t8-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # errors[t8-vectors].partitionErrors: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # errors[t8-vectors].partitionErrors[0].partition = 0
00 2f                                 # errors[t8-vectors].partitionErrors[0].errorCode = 47
```

### `addoffsetstotxn.request.v0`

AddOffsetsToTxn v0: the `__consumer_offsets` partition of `t8-vectors-group` joins the transaction of
`t8-vectors-txn`, so that the commit marker reaches it as well. The offsets themselves are not here.

<!-- vector: addoffsetstotxn.request.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 40                                       # messageSize = 64
00 19                                             # apiKey = 25
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 ba                                       # correlationId = 186
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 0e 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t8-vectors-txn"
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # producerId = 0
00 02                                             # producerEpoch = 2
00 10 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 67 72 6f   # groupId = "t8-vectors-group"
75 70
```

### `addoffsetstotxn.response.v0`

The answer of the transaction coordinator: one error code for the whole request, because this api enrols
exactly one partition and has nothing to report per partition.

<!-- vector: addoffsetstotxn.response.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 0a   # messageSize = 10
00 00 00 ba   # correlationId = 186
00 00 00 00   # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00         # errorCode = 0
```

### `endtxn.request.v0`

EndTxn v0 with `TransactionResult = 1`: commit the transaction of `t8-vectors-txn`. The single boolean
byte is the whole difference between a commit and an abort.

<!-- vector: endtxn.request.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 2f                                       # messageSize = 47
00 1a                                             # apiKey = 26
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 bd                                       # correlationId = 189
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 0e 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t8-vectors-txn"
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # producerId = 0
00 02                                             # producerEpoch = 2
01                                                # transactionResult = true
```

### `endtxn.response.v0`

The answer to a commit: the error code 0 means that the coordinator wrote its decision into
`__transaction_state`, not that the control batches are in the partitions.

<!-- vector: endtxn.response.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 0a   # messageSize = 10
00 00 00 bd   # correlationId = 189
00 00 00 00   # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00         # errorCode = 0
```

### `endtxn.request.v0.abort`

EndTxn v0 with `TransactionResult = 0`: abort the transaction of `t8-vectors-abort-txn`. Its records stay
in the log and are marked with an ABORT control batch.

<!-- vector: endtxn.request.v0.abort -->
```
00 00 00 35                                       # messageSize = 53
00 1a                                             # apiKey = 26
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 c6                                       # correlationId = 198
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 14 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 61 62 6f   # transactionalId = "t8-vectors-abort-txn"
72 74 2d 74 78 6e
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02                           # producerId = 2
00 00                                             # producerEpoch = 0
00                                                # transactionResult = false
```

### `endtxn.response.v0.abort`

The answer to an abort, the same frame a commit is answered with.

<!-- vector: endtxn.response.v0.abort -->
```
00 00 00 0a   # messageSize = 10
00 00 00 c6   # correlationId = 198
00 00 00 00   # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00         # errorCode = 0
```

### `endtxn.request.v0.invalid-state`

EndTxn v0 that aborts a transaction which was committed a moment before: the id is in `CompleteCommit`,
from which the coordinator allows a commit (again) but not an abort.

<!-- vector: endtxn.request.v0.invalid-state -->
```
00 00 00 2f                                       # messageSize = 47
00 1a                                             # apiKey = 26
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 be                                       # correlationId = 190
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 0e 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t8-vectors-txn"
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # producerId = 0
00 02                                             # producerEpoch = 2
00                                                # transactionResult = false
```

### `endtxn.response.v0.invalid-state`

The answer to that abort: the error code 48 (InvalidTxnState). A repeated **commit** of the same
transaction is answered with 0 instead.

<!-- vector: endtxn.response.v0.invalid-state -->
```
00 00 00 0a   # messageSize = 10
00 00 00 be   # correlationId = 190
00 00 00 00   # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 30         # errorCode = 48
```

### `writetxnmarkers.request.v0`

WriteTxnMarkers v0, the broker-to-broker request a transaction coordinator sends to the leader of every
partition of a finished transaction: write a COMMIT marker for the producer id 3 into
`t8-vectors-marker`-0, with the coordinator epoch of the `__transaction_state` partition it owns.

<!-- vector: writetxnmarkers.request.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 46                                       # messageSize = 70
00 1b                                             # apiKey = 27
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 c9                                       # correlationId = 201
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 00 00 01                                       # transactionMarkers: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 03                           # transactionMarkers[0].producerId = 3
00 00                                             # transactionMarkers[0].producerEpoch = 0
01                                                # transactionMarkers[0].transactionResult = true
00 00 00 01                                       # transactionMarkers[0].topics: 1 item(s)
00 11 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 6d 61 72   # transactionMarkers[0].topics[t8-vectors-marker].topic = "t8-vectors-marker"
6b 65 72
00 00 00 01                                       # transactionMarkers[0].topics[t8-vectors-marker].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # transactionMarkers[0].topics[t8-vectors-marker].partitions[0] = 0
00 00 00 00                                       # transactionMarkers[0].coordinatorEpoch = 0
```

### `writetxnmarkers.response.v0`

The answer of the partition leader: one entry per producer id, one error code per partition it appended
the control batch into. **The only answer of Kafka 0.11 that does not begin with a `ThrottleTimeMs`.**

<!-- vector: writetxnmarkers.response.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 31                                       # messageSize = 49
00 00 00 c9                                       # correlationId = 201
00 00 00 01                                       # transactionMarkers: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 03                           # transactionMarkers[0].producerId = 3
00 00 00 01                                       # transactionMarkers[0].topics: 1 item(s)
00 11 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 6d 61 72   # transactionMarkers[0].topics[t8-vectors-marker].topic = "t8-vectors-marker"
6b 65 72
00 00 00 01                                       # transactionMarkers[0].topics[t8-vectors-marker].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # transactionMarkers[0].topics[t8-vectors-marker].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00                                             # transactionMarkers[0].topics[t8-vectors-marker].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
```

### `txnoffsetcommit.request.v0`

TxnOffsetCommit v0, the only request of the transaction protocol that goes to the **group** coordinator:
the offset 2 of `t8-vectors`-0 is committed for `t8-vectors-group` inside the transaction of
`t8-vectors-txn`. There is no generation id, no member id and no retention time.

<!-- vector: txnoffsetcommit.request.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 62                                       # messageSize = 98
00 1c                                             # apiKey = 28
00 00                                             # apiVersion = 0
00 00 00 bc                                       # correlationId = 188
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 0e 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t8-vectors-txn"
00 10 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 67 72 6f   # groupId = "t8-vectors-group"
75 70
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # producerId = 0
00 02                                             # producerEpoch = 2
00 00 00 01                                       # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # topics[t8-vectors].topic = "t8-vectors"
00 00 00 01                                       # topics[t8-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topics[t8-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02                           # topics[t8-vectors].partitions[0].offset = 2
ff ff                                             # topics[t8-vectors].partitions[0].metadata = null
```

### `txnoffsetcommit.response.v0`

The answer of the group coordinator: the offset was written into `__consumer_offsets` as a transactional
record and stays invisible to an OffsetFetch of the group until the transaction is committed.

<!-- vector: txnoffsetcommit.response.v0 -->
```
00 00 00 22                           # messageSize = 34
00 00 00 bc                           # correlationId = 188
00 00 00 00                           # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00 00 01                           # topics: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73   # topics[t8-vectors].topic = "t8-vectors"
00 00 00 01                           # topics[t8-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                           # topics[t8-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00                                 # topics[t8-vectors].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
```

### `produce.request.v3.transactional`

Produce v3 of a transactional producer: the request carries the transactional id `t8-vectors-txn` - a
plain and an idempotent producer send `null` there - and bit 4 of the record batch attributes (0x10)
marks the batch itself as transactional.

<!-- vector: produce.request.v3.transactional -->
```
00 00 00 ab                                       # messageSize = 171
00 00                                             # apiKey = 0
00 03                                             # apiVersion = 3
00 00 00 b8                                       # correlationId = 184
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # clientId = "t8-vectors"
00 0e 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 74 78 6e   # transactionalId = "t8-vectors-txn"
ff ff                                             # requiredAcks = -1
00 00 13 88                                       # timeout = 5000
00 00 00 01                                       # topicMessages: 1 item(s)
00 0a 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73               # topicMessages[t8-vectors].topic = "t8-vectors"
00 00 00 01                                       # topicMessages[t8-vectors].partitions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00                                       # topicMessages[t8-vectors].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00 00 65 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 59   # topicMessages[t8-vectors].partitions[0].messageSet = 101 bytes
ff ff ff ff 02 48 b9 68 63 00 10 00 00 00 01 00
00 01 a0 86 6f 8f 71 00 00 01 a0 86 6f 8f 71 00
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02 00 00 00 00 00 00 00
02 26 00 00 00 01 1a 63 6f 6d 6d 69 74 74 65 64
20 6f 6e 65 00 26 00 00 02 01 1a 63 6f 6d 6d 69
74 74 65 64 20 74 77 6f 00
```

### `fetch.response.v5.aborted-transactions`

Fetch v5 answer of a `read_committed` consumer of a partition with one **aborted** transaction: the last
stable offset is 3, past everything, and `AbortedTransactions` names the producer id 2 with the first
offset 0. The record region nevertheless holds both aborted records and the ABORT control batch - the
broker names the transaction and leaves the filtering to the client.

<!-- vector: fetch.response.v5.aborted-transactions -->
```
00 00 01 07                                       # messageSize = 263
00 00 00 c7                                       # correlationId = 199
00 00 00 00                                       # throttleTimeMs = 0
00 00 00 01                                       # topics: 1 item(s)
00 10 74 38 2d 76 65 63 74 6f 72 73 2d 61 62 6f   # topics[t8-vectors-abort].topic = "t8-vectors-abort"
72 74

00 00 00 00                                       # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].partition = 0
00 00                                             # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].errorCode = 0
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 03                           # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].highWaterMarkOffset = 3
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 03                           # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].lastStableOffset = 3
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].logStartOffset = 0
00 00 00 01                                       # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].abortedTransactions: 1 item(s)
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02                           # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].abortedTransactions[0].producerId = 2
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00                           # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].abortedTransactions[0].firstOffset = 0
00 00 00 af 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 55   # topics[t8-vectors-abort].partitions[0].messageSet = 175 bytes
00 00 00 00 02 62 ee c1 c6 00 10 00 00 00 01 00
00 01 a0 86 6f 8f 71 00 00 01 a0 86 6f 8f 71 00
00 00 00 00 00 00 02 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00
02 22 00 00 00 01 16 61 62 6f 72 74 65 64 20 6f
6e 65 00 22 00 00 02 01 16 61 62 6f 72 74 65 64
20 74 77 6f 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02 00 00 00
42 00 00 00 00 02 69 a4 f2 7b 00 30 00 00 00 00
00 00 01 a0 86 6f 93 9e 00 00 01 a0 86 6f 93 9e
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02 00 00 ff ff ff ff 00 00
00 01 20 00 00 00 08 00 00 00 00 0c 00 00 00 00
00 00 00
```

### `messageformat.v2.control.abort`

The ABORT control batch that the transaction coordinator appended when the transaction of `t8-vectors-abort-txn`
was rolled back. It has the shape of the COMMIT marker `messageformat.v2.none.control` above and differs in one
byte: the type of the control record key is 0 instead of 1.

<!-- vector: messageformat.v2.control.abort -->
```
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02   # batches[0].baseOffset = 2
00 00 00 42               # batches[0].batchLength = 66
00 00 00 00               # batches[0].partitionLeaderEpoch = 0
02                        # batches[0].magic = 2
69 a4 f2 7b               # batches[0].crc = 1772417659 (CRC-32C of everything from attributes on)
00 30                     # batches[0].attributes = 0x0030 (codec none, CreateTime, transactional, control)
00 00 00 00               # batches[0].lastOffsetDelta = 0 (last offset 2)
00 00 01 a0 86 6f 93 9e   # batches[0].firstTimestamp = 1788961854366
00 00 01 a0 86 6f 93 9e   # batches[0].maxTimestamp = 1788961854366
00 00 00 00 00 00 00 02   # batches[0].producerId = 2
00 00                     # batches[0].producerEpoch = 0
ff ff ff ff               # batches[0].baseSequence = -1 (last sequence -1)
00 00 00 01               # batches[0].recordCount = 1
20                        # batches[0].records[0].length = 16
00                        # batches[0].records[0].attributes = 0
00                        # batches[0].records[0].timestampDelta = 0 (timestamp 1788961854366)
00                        # batches[0].records[0].offsetDelta = 0 (offset 2)
08                        # batches[0].records[0].key: 4 bytes
00 00                     # the control record key: version = 0
00 00                     # type = 0 (ABORT)
0c                        # batches[0].records[0].value: 6 bytes
00 00                     # the end transaction marker: version = 0
00 00 00 00               # coordinatorEpoch = 0
00                        # batches[0].records[0].headers: 0 item(s)
```
